**Current phase:** Phase 1B — Content Enhancement (Phase 1A + gates + Operations Console + Onboarding complete; Phase 1B session map authored by P1B-S0 — builds begin at P1B-S1)  
**Last updated:** 2026-09-05 (P1B-S2 — **Featured images SHIPPED: the post/page → media relationship, resolved on the delivery side.** **(1) The write-suppress trap, closed.** A featured-image change touches no other field, so if the canonical checksum ignored the reference, DECISION 3 would correctly suppress the upsert and the change would vanish. Compounding it, `_thumbnail_id` is `_`-prefixed, so `ProtectedMeta::publicOnly()` strips it — correctly, since the raw value is WordPress bookkeeping that must not be published. The reference is therefore extracted **deliberately** via a new `ProtectedMeta::featuredMediaId()` and carried as a **typed field** on the source and canonical models rather than smuggled through `meta`, and `featuredMediaId` is **appended to both canonical checksums**. Tests cover setting, replacing and clearing, on posts and pages. **CONSEQUENCE (expected, self-healing): every existing post/page checksum changes**, so each entity re-projects once on its next event; reconciliation would see the drift regardless. The two pinned-digest tests were **re-pinned by recomputing the documented algorithm independently**, not by pasting the new output — a pinned test that is updated from its own failure output proves nothing. **(2) Resolution: LEFT JOIN, one query, no N+1.** The listing joins `content.media` on `fm.source_post_id = p.featured_media_id AND fm.deleted_at IS NULL`, so a listing costs exactly **ONE** query at any page size (asserted: a one-row page and a full page issue the same number of queries). Read-time resolution was chosen over write-time denormalisation for a concrete reason, proven by test: **editing the attachment is visible to consumers without re-saving the post**, with no re-emission fan-out (one media edit would otherwise have to re-emit every referencing entity) and no stale window. **(3) A soft reference may always dangle — and does so safely.** `featured_media_id BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` on both projections, **no FK** (ADR-013: replay must be able to land a post before its attachment). All three no-image cases — none set, never projected, soft-deleted — resolve identically to `featured_media: null`; the post is still listed and still served, never a dangling id and never a 500. Proven on live PostgreSQL for each case, including a listing that mixes resolved, absent and dangling in one query. **(4) Index-backed at scale.** An `EXPLAIN` test seeds **2,000 media + 500 posts pointing at distinct attachments** and asserts no `Seq Scan on media`. The first draft seeded ONE media row and failed — correctly: against a one-row table the planner rightly prefers a seq scan + materialize over 500 index lookups, so the assertion was meaningless until both tables were realistically sized. Fixed the test, not the assertion. **Migration `0007_add_featured_media_to_content_entities`** (ALTER on both entity projections, no index — the join rides the existing `uq_content_media_source_post_id`; an index on `featured_media_id` would only serve a reverse lookup no endpoint offers). Registered in `ContentModule::getMigrations()` — and thanks to the **FLAG-P1BS1-1 fix landed earlier today, the onboarding gate picked it up automatically with NO `core/` edit**: GATE-S4 criterion 1 ("a new content field reaches the projection without touching `core/`") **passes for this session**. **Test-schema debt surfaced and contained:** six integration tests hand-copy content DDL, so the new column and join broke eleven test classes that care about neither. Rather than paste the DDL an eleventh time, a shared `tests/Support/ContentSchema` helper applies the **real migration files** additively (IF NOT EXISTS / ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS — it never redefines a schema a test built itself) and is called from each affected setUp. The remaining hand-copied blocks are pre-existing debt this session did not create; **recorded as OBSERVATION-P1BS2-1** rather than refactored mid-session. **Tests: Unit 1220/1220 green** (was 1201; +19 in the new `FeaturedImageTest`), 1 pre-existing skip; **Integration 161/161** on `hsp_test` (+7 in `FeaturedImageIntegrationTest`, which builds its schema from the real migration files); **PHPStan level 8 clean; PHPCS clean.** No new stage/worker/handle/`pg_*` wrapper/persistence; no second repair path; **no `tsvector`, no full-text index, no search endpoint** (DECISION Y). **FLAG-P1BS0-1 remains the only open flag** (60s cadence vs the <30s SLA — untouched). **Next session: P1B-S3 (tags).**)  
**Last updated:** 2026-09-05 (P1B-S1 follow-up — **FLAG-P1BS1-1 RESOLVED at the root: the onboarding migrations gate now DERIVES its module requirements from the module registry.** Option (c) of the three recorded, chosen over the cheap fix because the hand-maintained list was not just a scope breach — it was the same shape of gap that let migration `0011` ship unwired and unnoticed (FLAG-ONBS2-1): a module could ship a projection migration, have the engine apply it, and still leave the gate reporting "passed" on an install where the table was missing. **`MigrationsAppliedCheck` now has two halves:** `REQUIRED_CORE` keeps the pipeline-critical system migrations (core's own, changing only when core changes), and the module half is **derived at run time** from the module registry's declarative `getMigrations()` (OPEN-9 / DECISION W (e)) through an injected `callable(): list<MigrationInterface>`. `OnboardingServiceProvider` builds that collection once in a shared private helper and hands the SAME closure to both `MigrationApplier` (which APPLIES the migrations) and `MigrationsAppliedCheck` (which VERIFIES them), so **the gate can no longer check a different set from the one that runs**. **Rule 5 holds** — core imports no module migration class; it receives `MigrationInterface` instances the module constructed. Resolution is **lazy and defensive**: building module migrations opens the libpq DDL link eagerly and throws on an unconfigured site, so the check catches that and degrades to core-only rather than fatalling (ADR-054 Principle 8) — activation and the onboarding screen must never 500. **Net effect:** the Content module's **six** declared migrations are now all required (up from the three listed by hand), and **P1B-S2 and every later Phase 1B row that ships a projection migration is covered automatically — no `core/` edit and no Files-column amendment needed.** New shared test double `tests/Support/FakeModuleMigration` (name-only `MigrationInterface` stub + `listOf()` / `contentModule()` builders) replaces the hand-listed content migrations across five onboarding fixtures. **+2 new tests:** a declared-but-unapplied module migration hard-blocks the check; a module list that cannot be built degrades to core-only instead of throwing. **Unit 1201/1201 green** (was 1199), 1 pre-existing skip; **Integration 154/154**; **PHPStan level 8 clean; PHPCS clean.** No schema change, no new handle, no new `pg_*` wrapper, no contract change beyond the check's own constructor. **FLAG-P1BS0-1 remains the only open flag** (60s cadence vs the <30s SLA — untouched here). Next session unchanged: **P1B-S2 (featured images)**.)  
**Last updated:** 2026-09-05 (P1B-S1 — **Media synchronization SHIPPED: the `content.media` aggregate end-to-end (capture → relay → dispatch → projection → delivery)**. The first Phase 1B build session. A new first-class aggregate walking the **existing** Content spine with **no new infrastructure**: hook wiring → outbox → handler → extractor → source model → validator → transformer → canonical model → adapter → projection, plus query provider → resource → REST registration. **(1) Capture.** Attachment lifecycle is wired through the **dedicated attachment hooks** (`add_attachment`, `attachment_updated`, `edit_attachment`, `delete_attachment`) and explicitly **NOT** by widening `HookWiring::SUPPORTED_POST_TYPES` — that would route attachments into the post transformer, which knows nothing about MIME types, dimensions or size variants. `edit_attachment` is wired deliberately: an **alt-text change fires only that hook** (the post row does not change, so `attachment_updated` never fires) and would otherwise never reach consumers. WordPress fires several of these for one user action, so a per-request first-emit-wins guard (`$handledMedia`, separate from the post `$handledByTransition` map) collapses them to **one** outbox row — safe precisely because processing is state-sync (DECISION H / ADR-044): the single event reloads the final state at process time. **Deletion is exempt from the guard** — it is terminal, always emits, and blocks any later upsert emit for that id, so a create-then-delete in one request cannot leave the projection serving a file that no longer exists. **(2) Projection.** New `content.media` (migration `0006_create_content_media`) carrying the **transformed** delivery shape — source id, slug, MIME type, resolved URL, alt text, caption, description, dimensions, size variants, `attached_to_id` — never a `wp_posts`/`wp_postmeta` replica (Rule 2), with the frozen column canon (`id UUID` PK, `checksum VARCHAR(64)`, all timestamps `TIMESTAMPTZ`, `deleted_at`, `meta_jsonb`). **No status column**: attachments carry `post_status='inherit'`, outside the `{publish}` public set (OPEN-10), so membership is existence — the rule categories already use. **Size variants are resolved to absolute URLs write-side** by the transformer (WordPress stores bare filenames relative to the original), so a consumer never reconstructs an upload path — that would couple it to WP internals (Rule 6) — and the read path assembles nothing (Rule 2). **(3) Delivery.** `GET /hsp/v1/media` + `/hsp/v1/media/{slug}` through a `MediaQueryProvider`/`MediaResource` pair with the DECISION F cursor envelope, zero synchronous WordPress reads (Rule 6), and complete `EndpointDescriptor`s so the ADR-055 drift guard stays green and `openapi.json` picks them up with **no hand-authoring** (guard count 7 → 9: eight content + openapi.json). **(4) Reconciliation + replay.** `WpReconciliationSource::AGGREGATE_TYPES` and `ContentReplayEmitter::AGGREGATE_TYPES` both learn `media`, so a full reconcile and the onboarding backfill re-emit media through the normal pipeline — **without this the entire media library would have been silently invisible to backfill**. Attachments needed their own corpus pager (`listAttachmentIdsAfter`): the existing `listPostIdsAfter` filters `post_status='publish'`, which would have returned an empty media corpus forever. Unlike terms, attachments DO carry `post_modified_gmt`, so incremental detection (DECISION U D1) works on them. **PERFORMANCE DoD met (P1B-S0 requirement).** The migration ships a **partial composite index** `(published_at DESC, id DESC) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` matching the listing predicate and sort exactly, a partial slug index, an `updated_at` index and a GIN index on `meta_jsonb`. Two live `EXPLAIN` assertions over a **2,000-row seeded table with fresh ANALYZE** prove the listing and the slug lookup are **index-backed (no Seq Scan)**; a query-count test proves a listing costs **the same one query at any page size (no N+1)**; batch behaviour is unchanged (the media stage drains under the config-driven `processing.projection_batch_size` — no hard-coded batch, so cadence + batch size remain the only throughput levers, ADR-054 §4). A live cursor test pages 25 rows that **all share one `published_at`** — the case a naive cursor gets wrong — and proves every row is returned exactly once. **HARD constraints honoured:** no new stage, no new worker type, **no fifth PG handle** (DECISION L Ruling 0), **no new `pg_*` wrapper** (DECISION E), no new persistence (DECISION Q), no second repair path, constructor injection throughout (ADR-012); **no `tsvector`, no full-text index, no search endpoint** (DECISION Y). **Tests: Unit 1199/1199 green** (was 1134; +65 — MediaExtractor/MediaTransformer/CanonicalMedia/MediaAdapter/MediaQueryProvider suites, 10 new HookWiring media cases, 2 new replay cases), 1 pre-existing skip; **Integration 154/154** on the dedicated `hsp_test` PG database (+7 new in `MediaProjectionIntegrationTest`); **PHPStan level 8 clean; PHPCS clean.** The integration test builds its schema by executing the **REAL migration SQL file** rather than hand-copied DDL — hand-seeded DDL is exactly what let migration 0011 ship unwired and unnoticed (FLAG-ONBS2-1), and it means these tests also prove the migration and its indexes. **Two PHPStan findings in new code were FIXED rather than baselined** (a missing `CursorPage<...>` generic and a `json_encode(): string|false` into `base64_encode`) — the sibling providers baseline both, but each was a one-line real fix; three `$wpdb` baseline counts in `WpReconciliationSource` were bumped for the new attachment pager. **SCOPE DEVIATION — FLAGGED, see FLAG-P1BS1-1:** one `core/` file was touched (`core/Onboarding/Preflight/MigrationsAppliedCheck.php`) although the P1B-S1 Files column lists only `modules/Content/`, tests and docs. `0006_create_content_media` was added to the onboarding `REQUIRED` list because media hooks fire from activation, so without the table every media event would fail projection and land in the DLQ on a fresh install. This is the same class of justified out-of-scope edit ONB-S2 made to `database/`, and it is why GATE-S4 criterion 1 ("a new content field reaches the projection without touching `core/`") reports a dirty `core/` tree until commit. **No open questions; session pointer advanced — next session is P1B-S2 (featured images).**)

attachment lifecycle via the **dedicated attachment hooks**, NOT by widening `HookWiring::SUPPORTED_POST_TYPES`, which would route attachments through the post transformer) → **P1B-S2 featured images** (post/page → media **soft reference**, ADR-013 no FK, resolved on the delivery side; the DoD names the write-suppress trap explicitly — a `_thumbnail_id`-only change must move the canonical checksum or DECISION 3 correctly suppresses the write) → **P1B-S3 tags** (**no new table**: `content.taxonomies` already carries `taxonomy_type VARCHAR(50)` with a type index and `content.entity_taxonomies` is the generic join per the P1A-S4 ruling, so tags are rows with `taxonomy_type = 'post_tag'`; `FilterSet` gains a `tag` filter **additively**) → **P1B-S4 basic ACF** (**no schema change**: `meta_jsonb JSONB` with a GIN index already exists on both entity projections and GATE-S4 already proved the seam with zero `core/` changes; the DoD keeps ACF-absent a normal no-op and holds the AUDIT-ONB-OPSC-S1 protected-meta fix under regression test) → **P1B-S5 validation & contract close** (pagination workflows with duplicate sort keys, ADR-055 drift guard, GATE-S1..S4 re-run, backfill/reconcile write-spy; test/docs evidence only, a failing check is STOP-and-flag). **Authored against ADR-054** (ALIGN-S0 Deliverable 5): every new handler runs inside the **existing projection stage of the bounded cycle** — no new stage, no new worker type, no new PG handle, no new `pg_*` wrapper, no new persistence — and throughput acceptance is framed as cron-frequency + batch-size tuning. Guard-grep confirms **no daemon / supervisor / systemd / worker-pool / restart language in any build row** (the only occurrences are inside the P1B-S0 row's own prohibition clause), and **no row introduces a `tsvector` column, full-text index or search endpoint** (DECISION Y). **(3) Performance pass (added after review — the first draft of the map had no performance DoD at all).** Read the PRD, Doc 1 §3/§15, Doc 8 §10, Doc 10 §24 and Doc 11 §9–10 and sized every build row against the real targets: **<30s end-to-end sync SLA** (PRD §Performance; queue lag <60s per Doc 10 §24) and **100,000+ content records / 1,000,000+ events/day** (PRD §Scalability; Doc 1 §15 phases Phase 1 at 10,000 records). Each build row now carries an explicit **PERFORMANCE DoD**: index-backed access paths proven by `EXPLAIN` (**no sequential scan**), a hard **no-N+1 rule** (the query count for a one-row page must equal a full-size page — this is the real risk in P1B-S2's featured-image resolution and P1B-S3's tag join), **config-driven batch sizes only** (a hard-coded batch removes the platform's only throughput lever — ADR-054 §4), and a full default batch draining **inside `cycle_time_budget_seconds`**. P1B-S5 gains the **first end-to-end sync-latency measurement the platform has ever had**, recorded with the cadence and batch sizes alongside it. **(4) `docs/notes/PERFORMANCE-BRIEF.md` (NEW)** — a cited, explicitly non-authoritative digest of the latency and scale targets, the ADR-054 "cadence + batch size is the only lever" model, the shipped `config/worker.php` defaults, and the read/write-path speed rules, plus a six-question sizing checklist — so a later session can size a design decision **without re-reading the PRD, Doc 1, Doc 8, Doc 10 and Doc 11**. **(5) FLAG-P1BS0-1 RAISED (open, needs a ruling):** the shipped `processing.interval_seconds = 60` **cannot** meet a <30s SLA worst case — the wait for the next cycle alone is 0–60s before any work starts — and ordinary content saves do **not** spawn a cycle (`spawn_cron()` is called only by the onboarding remediation endpoint), so on a quiet site WP-Cron makes it worse. Compounding it, **no test measures end-to-end sync latency anywhere**, so the SLA is an aspiration rather than a verified property. Four options recorded (tighten the default / restate the measurement basis / make it an operator tuning obligation / spawn on capture); **not resolved here** — ADR-054 is not reopened, and no build session may change the cadence on its own authority. **Pagination is not dropped:** cursor pagination shipped in P1A-S5 (DECISION F / Doc 9 §13), so the Phase 1B obligation is that every new list surface carries the same envelope — validated in P1B-S5 rather than in a session of its own, stated explicitly in a note under the map so it cannot read as an omission. **One flag open (FLAG-P1BS0-1); session pointer advanced — next session is P1B-S1.**)  
**Last updated:** 2026-09-05 (LAZYPG-S1 — **lazy PostgreSQL connections at the container boundary SHIPPED; DECISION Z ratified (ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.31); both pre-Phase-1B carry-forwards CLOSED**. Interstitial before P1B-S0, resolving the ONB-S1b "lazy-connection ruling pre-Phase-1B" that came due. **The defect:** `PostgresDatabaseConnection` required an already-open handle, so all four runtime PG providers called `pg_connect()` **inside their singleton factory** and let a raw `\RuntimeException` escape when PostgreSQL was unreachable or unconfigured. `ContentModule::boot()` correctly defers registrar construction to `rest_api_init` (FLAG-P1AS6-2 Gap C), but that hook fires on **every REST request to the site** — `wp/v2` and the block editor included — and building the registrar resolves the query providers ([ContentServiceProvider.php:85](headless-sync/modules/Content/ContentServiceProvider.php#L85)), which opened the socket: an unreachable PostgreSQL fatalled **every REST request**, not just `hsp/v1` (ADR-054 Principle 8 violation on an unconfigured site). **The fix — the idiom already in the tree:** the capture path had been hotfixed the same way, so this mirrors `MysqliOutboxConnection`'s connector closure onto the PostgreSQL side, once, where all four callers route through. `PostgresDatabaseConnection` now accepts a handle **or** a `\Closure(): handle` CONNECTOR, invokes it **at most once** on first real use (`execute`/`query`/`beginTransaction`/`commit`), memoizes the handle, and translates any connect failure to `DatabaseException` at that boundary — so `PgsqlOutboxConnection`/`DatabaseQueueConnection` keep translating one exception type onward to `OutboxWriteException`/`QueueException` (DECISION E v1.6) instead of an arbitrary throwable escaping a container factory. `rollback()` on a connection that was **never opened is a no-op** — it must not dial the socket to undo a transaction that cannot exist (the DECISION E v1.6 never-throws semantics are preserved). The four providers (`Delivery`/`Queue`/`Dispatcher`/`Outbox`) pass connectors instead of connecting. **HARD constraints honoured:** still **four handles**, each with its existing flags — `PGSQL_CONNECT_FORCE_NEW` on delivery/queue-claim/dispatcher, none on the relay handle — so **DECISION K isolation and DECISION L Ruling 0 topology are untouched**; **no fifth handle, no new `pg_*` wrapper** (DECISION E), no persistence (DECISION Q), no schema change, no migration, no contract change, constructor injection intact (ADR-012). The migration engine's DDL-only `ConnectionFactory` is deliberately **out of scope** (reached only from explicit migrate actions, which already gate on reachability — DECISION W (f)). **Tests (+10 Unit):** five connector cases on `PostgresDatabaseConnectionTest` (no connect at construction; `rollback()` does not open; connector failure → `DatabaseException` not `\RuntimeException`; non-handle return rejected; invocation count) and a new `tests/Unit/Container/LazyPgConnectionResolutionTest` that registers all four providers and resolves every PG binding **with no `HSP_PG_*` configured at all** — the sharpest form of the guard, since `CredentialResolver::pgHost()` throws for a missing credential, so any DSN read at resolution time fails the test — plus a four-distinct-objects topology assertion. **Suite: Unit 1134/1134 green** (was 1124), 1 pre-existing skip; **Integration 147/147** on the dedicated `hsp_test` PG database; **PHPStan level 8 clean; PHPCS clean.** **Second carry-forward CLOSED with no code change:** the `global $wpdb` reach in the outbox provider closures ([OutboxServiceProvider.php:93,99,104](headless-sync/core/Container/Definitions/OutboxServiceProvider.php#L93)) is **not** an ADR-012 violation — ADR-012 prohibits service-locator calls **inside business logic**, and these sit in the **composition root**, the correct place to read a WordPress global (the provider's own header says so); the only real cost was test ergonomics (ALIGN-S2's `$GLOBALS['wpdb']` stub), which does not justify churning a constructor signature used across the suite. Recorded, closed, not carried forward again. **No open flags; session pointer advanced — next session is P1B-S0**, which additionally carries the user's 2026-09-05 decision to **defer PostgreSQL full-text search out of Phase 1B to Phase 5** as **DECISION Y** (reserved letter).)

Both surfaces (`wp hsp status` and the console) now implement the ratified definition against the same config-driven `offline_after_seconds` the health/worker-status providers use; the ratified `OperabilityValidationTest` §4 assertion was UPDATED to §6 semantics (1 in the window, 2 in a widened window) rather than bypassed. Replay now names tombstones in its summary, so a mistyped aggregate_id in the console Replay form is visible instead of reading as success — the emission itself is correct WordPress-wins behaviour and was deliberately left intact. Unit **1124/1124**, Integration **147/147**, PHPStan level 8 clean, PHPCS clean. **No open flags; session pointer NOT advanced — next session remains P1B-S0.**)  
**Last updated:** 2026-09-05 (AUDIT-ONB-OPSC-S1 — **live audit of the hsp-onboarding + hsp-operations surfaces against the ADR-054 cron model, all findings FIXED**. Six defects found on the real site and repaired: activation-time full reconciliation that pre-empted the onboarding backfill (reconciliation events now start one interval out, not at `time()`); MySQL migrations skipped forever after a WordPress-DB restore because their ledger lives in PostgreSQL (migrations may now report `isSatisfied()`; the runner re-applies a missing artifact); the Operations dashboard rendering twice per request (duplicate same-slug submenu callback); the Replay/Reconcile endpoint having no UI at all (new `ActionsView` + JS client — DECISION V (d)); the 15s poll fetching snapshots and discarding them (now re-renders through the same `DashboardView`); and `system.worker_heartbeats` growing one row per cycle unbounded and rendered in full (retention prune in the maintenance stage + capped console read + honest `worker_count`). Also: protected WordPress meta (`_edit_lock`/`_edit_last`) no longer leaks over the public delivery API (dropped at extraction); the onboarding menu now hides once complete; onboarding heading/button contrast fixed (wp-admin `h1` colour beat inheritance; Tailwind preflight is disabled so buttons fell back to UA defaults) and `dist/` rebuilt. Unit **1121/1121** green (+24 new), Integration **147/147** on a dedicated `hsp_test` DB, PHPStan level 8 clean, PHPCS clean. **Two flags open for ruling (FLAG-AUDIT1/2); session pointer NOT advanced — next session remains P1B-S0.**)

Product ruling (authority): **HSP v1.x supports ONLY WP-Cron for background execution — no Supervisor, systemd, Docker workers, CLI daemons, or continuously running processes.** The execution *mechanism* changes; nothing else does. **Doc 8 → v2.0 "Background Processing & Execution Architecture"** (retitled): complete rewrite around a **WP-Cron Processing Engine** — `WP-Cron tick → bootstrap → relay batch → dispatch batch → projection batch → maintenance → persist operational metrics → clean exit`; **stateless between executions**; **per-stage config-driven max batch sizes + execution-time budget + continuation across cron runs** (`config/worker.php`: `processing.relay_batch_size`/`dispatch_batch_size`/`projection_batch_size`/`cycle_time_budget_seconds`, **no schema change**). **Preserved unchanged:** the Outbox→Relay→Dispatcher→Queue→Processing pipeline, per-event pipeline (Claim→Load→Context→Validate→Resolve→Execute→Commit→Ack), execution context, subscribers/handlers, registry-based resolution, aggregate-version ordering (DECISION J), visibility timeout (OPEN-4/DECISION R), replay (DECISION T), reconciliation (DECISION U), correlation/causation IDs (ADR-037), failure isolation, stateless processing, at-least-once + idempotent redelivery, four-connection topology (DECISION L Ruling 0), heartbeat current-state (DECISION P), metrics/progress without persistence (DECISION Q). **Removed:** long-running workers, supervisors, systemd/container restart policies, worker recycling/startup/shutdown lifecycle, multi-process worker pools, daemon health monitoring, heartbeat-as-liveness. **Concurrency:** overlapping cron cycles proven safe via **existing guarantees ONLY** (SKIP LOCKED + aggregate versioning + visibility timeout + DECISION 3 atomic commit) — the two required scenarios (two overlapping cycles claiming the same queue; a cycle killed mid-batch recovering via visibility timeout next cycle) analysed explicitly; **NO new locking mechanism introduced**, and the explicit gap check found no gap requiring one (a single-flight lock is deliberately NOT added — would require a new DECISION). **Health** = processing freshness/progress (last cron execution, last successful cycle, per-stage last-run, queue depth, oldest pending job, processing lag). **Metrics** = cycles-completed / avg-cycle-duration / per-stage-throughput / queue-backlog / processing-lag; `worker_uptime`/`restart_count` **removed**. **Naming:** implementation class names retained (`WorkerEngine`, `RelayWorkerStrategy`, `EventWorkerStrategy`, `ReconciliationWorkerStrategy`, `MaintenanceWorkerStrategy`) — defined as processing components invoked by WP-Cron; Added `spawn_cron` stub to `tests/bootstrap.php`. **Suite: Unit 1072/1072 green, 0 deprecations** (was 1053; +19 net incl. the migrate/spawn/guard tests and `CoreMigrationsListTest`), 1 pre-existing skip; Integration 147 loads clean, DB tests self-skip without env. **FRESH-INSTALL E2E on the live Local site (real WordPress + real `$wpdb` + live PG `hsp`, state reset — `hsp_onboarding_state` deleted, PG `system`+`content` schemas dropped):** drove the ACTUAL controller flow (the same code the wp-admin REST endpoints call) — preflight (4 checks) → `handleMigrate` (**20 migrations applied**, migrations gate PASS) → worker gate correctly BLOCKS pre-cycle → `handleSpawnWorker` (non-blocking spawn) → one real Processing Engine cycle produces a heartbeat → both gates PASS ("cycle ran 0s ago") → `handleBackfill` (reconcileFull re-emitted 27 aggregates) → one cron cycle drains (relayed 27 → dispatched 27 → projected 27) → converged, `hsp_onboarding_state = complete`, **redirect = operations** — **ZERO manual CLI/engine steps → PASS ✓**. **This E2E surfaced + fixed a PRE-EXISTING latent bug** (see below). **HARD constraints:** no schema change beyond running EXISTING migrations; no OPSC/pipeline changes; no new PG handle / `pg_*` wrapper; no second repair path; DECISION W (c) no-in-request-drain preserved; constructor injection (ADR-012). robocopy to Local site done. `MigrationApplier` is non-`final` solely to allow the `SpyMigrationApplier` controller-boundary test double. **PRE-EXISTING BUG FOUND + FIXED (FLAG-ONBS2-1):** the fresh-install E2E's first real cron cycle crashed with "no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification" on `system.queue_jobs`. Root cause: migration **`0011_add_unique_event_id_to_queue_jobs`** (the `UNIQUE(event_id)` the dispatcher's `enqueueIdempotent()` `ON CONFLICT(event_id)` depends on — DECISION L v1.12) shipped in P1A-S6d as a **raw SQL file with NO PHP migration class** and was **never wired into `MigrationServiceProvider::migrations.core`**, so the engine never applied it on a real fresh install (integration tests seed schema directly, masking the gap). Fix (in scope — "run the EXISTING migrations"): added `database/Core/Pgsql/AddUniqueEventIdToQueueJobsMigration.php` wrapping the EXISTING frozen SQL (no schema change) + wired it into `migrations.core` (sorts after 0003) + added it to `MigrationsAppliedCheck::REQUIRED` (pipeline-critical) + a `CoreMigrationsListTest` regression guard (asserts every pipeline-critical migration is in the delegate list). Updated the affected migration-list fixtures (BackfillGate/BackfillService/OnboardingRestController/PreflightChecks/BackfillIntegration) to include 0011. Post-fix E2E re-run = PASS. **Scope note:** `database/` was not in the literal in-scope list — flagged, added only because it blocks the DoD and wraps an existing frozen migration. `git status` shows docs + STATUS + `headless-sync/{bootstrap,core,modules,resources,tests,database}/`.)

**Next session:** **P1B-S3 — Tags: the `post_tag` taxonomy through the existing taxonomy projection.** **No new table and no projection migration:** `content.taxonomies` already carries `taxonomy_type VARCHAR(50)` with a type index, and `content.entity_taxonomies` is the generic entity↔term join whose composite PK the P1A-S4 architect ruling froze (no timestamps, no checksum, no metadata — do not add columns to it). Tags are rows with `taxonomy_type = 'post_tag'`. The work is: three `content.tag.*` event constants; widening the term hooks past the hard-coded `taxonomy !== 'category'` bail to a supported-taxonomy set (an unsupported taxonomy must still be ignored); `tag` added to `WpReconciliationSource::AGGREGATE_TYPES` and `ContentReplayEmitter` (or reconcile/backfill silently skips tags — the same trap media hit); an **additive** `tag` filter on `core/Contracts/FilterSet` (Rule 5 — core owns the contract; no existing field removed, no existing consumer broken); and `GET /hsp/v1/posts?tag=…` plus a tags listing with complete `EndpointDescriptor`s. **Performance DoD:** the tag-filtered listing must be index-backed in **both** directions — `idx_content_entity_taxonomies_taxonomy_id` for term→entities and the PK for the hop back to `content.posts` — proven by an `EXPLAIN` assertion with **both tables realistically sized** (P1B-S2 learned this the hard way: against a tiny table the planner correctly prefers a seq scan and the assertion proves nothing); the cursor envelope with its `{"s","id"}` tiebreaker must survive the join; and returning a post's tags must not issue one query per post (**no N+1** — assert equal query counts for a one-row and a full-size page, as `FeaturedImageTest` does). **Read `docs/notes/PERFORMANCE-BRIEF.md` first.** **No `tsvector`, no full-text index, no search endpoint** (DECISION Y). **FLAG-P1BS0-1 is open** (60s cadence vs <30s SLA): do **not** change `processing.interval_seconds`. Note **OBSERVATION-P1BS2-1**: several integration tests still hand-copy content DDL; if P1B-S3 breaks them, prefer extending `tests/Support/ContentSchema` over pasting DDL again. See IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN §5b P1B-S3.

The FLAG-P1BS1-1 fix paid off immediately — the new migration was picked up by the onboarding gate with no `core/` edit, so GATE-S4 criterion 1 passed for this session.)*

One flag raised: **FLAG-P1BS1-1** (a justified `core/` touch outside the row's Files column).)*

The second carry-forward — the `global $wpdb` reach in the outbox provider closures — is **CLOSED, no code change**: ADR-012 prohibits service-locator calls **inside business logic**, and these three reads sit in the **composition root**, which is the correct place to read a WordPress global (the provider's own docblock says so). The only real cost was test ergonomics (ALIGN-S2 stubbed `$GLOBALS['wpdb']`), which does not justify churning a constructor signature used across the suite. Not carried forward again.)*

Every row is authored against the ADR-054 bounded-cycle model: new handlers run inside the **existing projection stage**, and throughput acceptance is cron-frequency + batch-size tuning. **Blockers: none** (one flag OPEN but non-blocking: **FLAG-P1BS0-1** — the shipped 60s cycle cadence vs the PRD <30s sync SLA, plus the absence of any end-to-end latency measurement; P1B-S5 takes the measurement and reports there, and **no build session may change `processing.interval_seconds` on its own authority**). FLAG-DOC8V2-1 closed by DOC-RECON-S1; both pre-Phase-1B carry-forwards closed by LAZYPG-S1 (the eager-connection item by DECISION Z; the `global $wpdb` item as a no-change composition-root ruling). **PostgreSQL full-text search is OUT of Phase 1B** — **DECISION Y** (ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.32; Doc 11 → v1.2, superseded entries bannered not deleted) defers it to **Phase 5 — Search Expansion**; no Phase 1B session may introduce a `tsvector` column, a full-text index, or a search endpoint. Doc 9 §14/§15, Doc 3 §27 and Doc 11 §14/§17 are untouched, and the §17 search-roadmap ordering is unchanged. Post-review refinements applied (contract shape = ruling not design decision → T1a BLOCKED; T4 BLOCKED on FLAG-ALIGN-1 feasibility). STEP 0 bookkeeping only (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN §5b ALIGN-S0 row + this item). Next = ALIGN-S1 alignment-implementation, pending architect rulings — does NOT advance to Phase 1B.

### GATE-S2 — Scalability Validation checklist (evidence: `tests/Integration/Gate/ScalabilityValidationTest.php`)

| # | §4 Scalability criterion | Result | Evidence (named integration test) |

| 1 | Multiple concurrent worker processes claim jobs without collision (SKIP LOCKED verified) | **PASS** | Two live-PG tests: `test_criterion1a_concurrent_sessions_skip_locked_rows_without_blocking_or_double_claiming` — two independent PG sessions (physical links, `PGSQL_CONNECT_FORCE_NEW`) each hold a `FOR UPDATE` lock on a queue row at the SAME instant; session B runs the exact OPEN-4 claim SELECT while A holds its lock and gets a DIFFERENT row (no double-claim); B carries a 2 s `statement_timeout` so blocking on A's row would FAIL instead of hang (proves no claimant blocks); a third session skips BOTH locked rows (two claims genuinely concurrent, not serialized). `test_criterion1b_real_provider_claims_every_job_exactly_once_across_workers` — 12 jobs, 3 distinct `DatabaseQueueProvider` workers on 3 physical connections, round-robin `claim()`; every job claimed exactly once, claimed-set equals the whole queue, ≥2 workers participate, no PG-side double-claim. |
| 2 | Queue growth handled without head-blocking | **PASS** | `test_criterion2_stuck_head_job_does_not_block_other_ready_jobs` — worker A claims the HEAD job (earliest `available_at`) with a 10 h visibility timeout so it is stuck/held; worker B on its own connection claims and COMPLETES all 6 followers past the stuck head; B never claims the head; head stays `claimed` by A; only the head remains claimed. SKIP LOCKED skips the head's lock rather than waiting on it. |
| 3 | Replay under load does not corrupt normal processing | **PASS** | `test_criterion3_replay_interleaved_with_normal_processing_stays_correct` — replay (DECISION T `ReplayService::replayEntity`) fired for a corrupted aggregate group WHILE a normal-load batch (4 posts + 2 pages) is mid-flight in the same outbox → relay → dispatch → queue; all drained interleaved in one worker loop. Asserts: both replayed and normally-processed aggregates converge to correct final projection state; `aggregate_versions` strictly monotonic (replay advanced via fresh DECISION 2 counter, normal-load at v1, none regressed); one projection row per aggregate (no forks); zero duplicate `system.processed_events` (PK-keyed; distinct == total). |

## Ops Notes

**CI — OpenAPI meta-schema gate (OAPI-S1 / ADR-055 (f)(2)).** The drift-guard meta-schema assertion runs via the Node ajv validator in `tools/openapi-validator/`. It is **layered over an always-run PHP structural pre-check**; the ajv gate itself **skips** when a `node` runtime is unavailable **unless** `HSP_REQUIRE_NODE_GATE=1` is set, in which case a missing node runtime is a **hard failure** (never a silent skip). **There is no CI workflow file in the repo yet.** When CI is added, its OpenAPI/test job MUST:
1. `cd headless-sync/tools/openapi-validator && npm ci` (installs the pinned ajv + ajv-formats from the committed `package-lock.json`; `node_modules/` is gitignored),
2. export `HSP_REQUIRE_NODE_GATE=1` for the PHPUnit run,
so the gate executes at full fidelity and cannot degrade to a skip. Locally, the gate runs automatically when node + `npm install` are present; it skips (with a warning naming the env var) otherwise.

## Flags

### OBSERVATION-P1BS2-1 — Six integration tests hand-copy content DDL instead of applying the real migrations

**Raised:** 2026-09-05 | **Session:** P1B-S2 (featured images) | **Status:** **OPEN — an observation, not a blocker. No ruling needed; a cleanup session would close it.**

**What.** `AdapterAtomicityIntegrationTest`, `DeliveryApiIntegrationTest`, `HandlerSpineIntegrationTest`, `ResolveStageGuardIntegrationTest` and `DeliveryConnectionIsolationTest` each build `content.pages` / `content.posts` from **hand-copied DDL** in their own `createSchema()`. Every schema change therefore has to be duplicated into each of them by hand.

**Why it matters.** This is the same drift that let migration `0011` ship unwired and unnoticed (FLAG-ONBS2-1): the tests passed against hand-seeded tables while a real fresh install had no such constraint. P1B-S2 felt the cost directly — adding one column and one join broke **eleven** test classes that care about neither.

**What P1B-S2 did instead of pasting the DDL an eleventh time.** Added `tests/Support/ContentSchema::ensureFeaturedMediaSupport()`, which applies the **real migration files** and is deliberately **additive** (`IF NOT EXISTS` / `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`), so it can be called from an existing `setUp()` without disturbing whatever schema that test builds for itself. Eleven tests now call it. Newer tests (`MediaProjectionIntegrationTest`, `FeaturedImageIntegrationTest`) build their whole schema from the real migration files and need no such patching.

**The remaining debt** is the five hand-copied blocks themselves — pre-existing, not created by this session. A cleanup would convert them to build from the real migration files, deleting the duplicated DDL. Deliberately NOT done mid-session: it touches five unrelated test files and would bury a featured-images diff.

**Guidance for the next session:** if P1B-S3 breaks these tests again, extend `ContentSchema` rather than pasting DDL a further time.

### FLAG-P1BS1-1 — P1B-S1 touched one `core/` file outside its Files column (onboarding required-migrations list)

**Raised:** 2026-09-05 | **Session:** P1B-S1 (media synchronization) | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (same day, separate follow-up commit) — option (c), the durable fix: `MigrationsAppliedCheck` now DERIVES its module requirements from the module registry, so no module session ever has to edit it again.**

**What was flagged.** P1B-S1 added `0006_create_content_media` to a hand-maintained `REQUIRED` constant in `core/Onboarding/Preflight/MigrationsAppliedCheck.php`, although the row's Files column listed only `modules/Content/`, tests and docs. Three options were recorded: (a) accept and amend the Files column, (b) revert and accept a gate that stops verifying new projection tables, (c) derive `REQUIRED` from the module registry.

**Resolution — option (c).** The required set now has two halves:

- **CORE** — `REQUIRED_CORE`, the pipeline-critical system migrations (events / queue / `0011` unique event id / aggregate versions / processed events / schema versions). These are core's own and change only when core changes.
- **MODULES** — **derived at run time** from the module registry's declarative `getMigrations()` (OPEN-9 / DECISION W (e)) via an injected `callable(): list<MigrationInterface>`, the SAME lazy collection `MigrationApplier` already applies. `OnboardingServiceProvider` now builds that collection once in a shared private helper and hands it to both, so **the gate can never verify a different set from the one that runs** — which was the underlying drift, not just the scope breach.

**Why this is the right half to fix.** The hand-maintained list had the same shape as the gap that let migration `0011` ship unwired and unnoticed (FLAG-ONBS2-1): a module could ship a projection migration, have the engine apply it, and still leave the gate reporting "passed" on an install where the table was missing. Deriving makes the gate self-maintaining and strictly stronger — every migration a module declares is now required, on the principle that a module declaring a migration needs it.

**Rule 5 holds.** Core imports no module migration class; it receives `MigrationInterface` instances each module constructed. Resolution is lazy and defensive: building module migrations opens the libpq DDL link eagerly and throws on an unconfigured site, so the check catches that and degrades to core-only rather than fatalling — activation and the onboarding screen must never 500 (ADR-054 Principle 8). Both behaviours are covered by new tests (`a module migration missing from the database blocks the check`; `the check degrades to core only when module migrations cannot be built`).

**Consequence for Phase 1B.** P1B-S2 and every later row that ships a projection migration is covered automatically — **no Files-column amendment and no `core/` edit needed**. The Content module's six declared migrations are now all required, up from the three that were listed by hand.

**Verified:** Unit **1201/1201** green (+2), Integration **154/154**, PHPStan level 8 clean, PHPCS clean.


### FLAG-P1BS0-1 — The shipped 60s cycle cadence cannot meet the PRD <30s sync SLA, and no test measures sync latency

**Raised:** 2026-09-05 | **Session:** P1B-S0 (Phase 1B planning) | **Status:** **OPEN — needs an architect/product ruling. Do NOT resolve it inside a build session.**

**The tension.** The PRD commits to a hard number: *"Content updates should appear in delivery systems within 30 seconds"* (PRD §Performance, restated in §Success Criteria; carried as "the <30s sync SLA" in Doc 8 §10, Doc 10 §24, Doc 11 §24, DECISION L (g) and ADR-054 §4). The shipped default is `config/worker.php` → `processing.interval_seconds = **60**`. Under ADR-054 a bounded cycle runs relay → dispatch → project in one invocation, so an event does traverse the whole pipeline in a single cycle — but **the wait for that cycle to start is 0–60s before any work happens**, so the worst case exceeds the SLA and the median sits right at it. Ordinary content saves do **not** trigger a cycle: `spawn_cron()` is called only by the onboarding remediation endpoint (`core/Onboarding/WorkerCronSpawner.php`), never from the capture path. On a low-traffic site the effective cadence is worse still, because WP-Cron only fires on a request unless a system cron drives `wp cron event run --due-now` (ADR-054 §5).

**Second half of the flag: there is no measurement.** No test in `tests/Integration/` asserts end-to-end sync latency. The Phase 1A DoD listed "Sync delay < 30s" and the P1A-S6 log records it as blocked at the time; nothing since has measured it. The SLA is currently an aspiration, not a verified property — so the cadence question cannot even be answered with data today.

**What is NOT in question.** ADR-054 is not being reopened: cadence and batch size are the only throughput levers (Doc 8 §10), overlapping cycles are safe under `SKIP LOCKED`, and no daemon/supervisor/worker-pool option exists. This flag is about the **default value** and **how the SLA is stated and measured**, nothing else.

**Options for the ruling (not chosen here):**
- **(a)** Tighten the shipped default `interval_seconds` so the worst case fits inside 30s, accepting more cron invocations on quiet sites.
- **(b)** Restate the SLA's measurement basis (e.g. p50, or "from cycle start"), so the documented target matches what a cron-driven model can guarantee.
- **(c)** Keep the 60s default but make the SLA an explicit **operator tuning obligation**, documented in the deployment runbook with a required system-cron cadence.
- **(d)** Emit a non-blocking `spawn_cron()` on capture so an edit starts a cycle immediately — a real design change with its own load implications, and it must not become an in-request drain (DECISION W (c)).

**Interim handling (already applied in P1B-S0):** every Phase 1B build row's performance DoD is written against cadence-independent properties (index-backed access paths, no N+1, config-driven batch sizes, a full batch draining inside `cycle_time_budget_seconds`), and **P1B-S5 takes the first end-to-end latency measurement** — recording the cadence and batch sizes alongside the number. If the SLA is missed at the shipped default, P1B-S5 **STOPS and reports here** rather than changing the cadence on its own authority. Digest of the targets and defaults: `docs/notes/PERFORMANCE-BRIEF.md`.

### FLAG-DOCRECON-1 — Nine OAPI-S1 PHP files are CRLF-terminated; `composer lint` is no longer baseline-green

**Raised:** 2026-09-05 | **Session:** DOC-RECON-S1 | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-09-05 (same session, separate follow-up commit).** **Root cause was not the files** — `git cat-file` proves every blob in HEAD was **already LF**; the CRLF existed only in the *working copy*, produced on checkout by the developer's global `core.autocrlf=true`. Converting the nine files would therefore have committed nothing and the drift would have returned on the next clone. **Fix: a repo-level `.gitattributes`** (`* text=auto` plus `text eol=lf` for `*.php`/`*.md`/`*.json`/`*.neon`/`*.xml`/`*.yml`/`*.yaml`/`*.js`/`*.ts`/`*.tsx`/`*.css`/`*.sql`, `binary` for `*.phar` and image/font types) — it overrides `core.autocrlf` per checkout, so working copies are LF on every machine and in CI, making `composer lint` reproducible. `phpcbf` normalised the nine stale working copies (formatting only — never a WPCS security sniff), and `git add --renormalize .` confirmed **zero content change** anywhere in the tree: the only committed file is `.gitattributes`. Verified after the fix: **PHPCS clean (0 errors, 0 warnings)**, **PHPStan level 8 `[OK] No errors`**, full suite **1244 tests / 3379 assertions green, 0 skips** on live MySQL + live PostgreSQL with `HSP_REQUIRE_NODE_GATE=1`. Original flag text follows. — **OPEN — NON-BLOCKING, cosmetic; out of scope for a docs-only session.** `vendor/bin/phpcs` reports **9 errors in 9 files**, all the identical `Generic.Files.LineEndings.InvalidEOLChar` ("expected `\n` but found `\r\n`") on line 1: `core/Contracts/Operations/{EndpointAuth,EndpointParameter,SchemaObject}.php`, `core/Operations/OpenApi/{OpenApiEndpointProvider,OpenApiGenerator,OpenApiRestController,OpenApiRestRegistrar}.php`, `core/Rest/RestRegistrarRegistry.php`, `modules/Content/Operations/ContentEndpointProvider.php` — i.e. exactly the files added or rewritten by **OAPI-S1**, written with Windows line endings. **Pre-existing:** this session modified only `docs/*` and `STATUS.md` (`git status` proves it), so the violations predate it and contradict the OAPI-S1 note "PHPStan L8 + PHPCS clean". PHPStan is unaffected (`[OK] No errors` at `--memory-limit=1G`; the default 256M limit crashes a parallel worker on this machine — environmental, not a code defect). **Resolution:** one `composer lint:fix` run (PHPCBF fixes all nine automatically — formatting only, never a WPCS security sniff), ideally paired with a `.gitattributes` `*.php text eol=lf` rule so it cannot recur. Deferred rather than done here to keep this session docs-only; touching nine PHP files would break that guarantee.

### FLAG-OAPI-2 — OAPI-S1 drift-guard route enumeration: delivery-contract surface only, or the full `hsp/v1` REST index?

**Raised:** 2026-07-20 | **Session:** OAPI-S1 (build) | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (architect ruling "A-modified"; ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.28).** ADR-055 (f)(1) says the drift guard asserts completeness over "every registered `hsp/v1` route," but six **onboarding** routes (`GET/POST hsp/v1/onboarding/*`) are also registered under `hsp/v1` — non-public admin first-run JSON endpoints (nonce+capability; DECISION W (a)/(e)), not delivery-API endpoints, carrying no `EndpointDescriptor` and out of scope for this session. The OAPI-S1 DoD counts exactly **seven** routes (six content + `openapi.json`). **Ruling (A-modified):** the guard enumerates the **FULL live `hsp/v1` route index** (external ground truth — preserves non-circularity), then subtracts exactly **ONE structural exemption** — the **`hsp/v1/onboarding/` prefix** (authority DECISION W (e): first-run admin surface outside the published delivery contract, registrar gated pre-completion). Every non-exempted route must carry a complete descriptor. The exemption is a **single prefix frozen in ADR-055 (f)** — adding any further exempt prefix needs an architect ruling; the guard hardcodes this one prefix with an ADR-055 (f) citation comment. Any future authenticated `hsp/v1` route **outside** the exempted prefix must carry a descriptor (`auth=authenticated` → excluded from the served document per v1.27; asserted by the exclusion test). **Net: 13 − 6 = 7 guarded routes**, matching the DoD. Added obligation: a named non-circularity test (a fixture `hsp/v1` route outside the exempted prefix without a descriptor fails the guard). ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.27→**v1.28** (amendment-log row + ADR-055 (f)(1) amended + drift-guard Implications row refreshed).

### FLAG-OAPI-1 — `GET /hsp/v1/openapi.json` scoping: public endpoints only, or the full contract behind a capability check?

**Raised:** 2026-07-20 | **Session:** OAPI-S0 (ADR-055 ratification) | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (ADR-055 (d) ruling; architect 2026-07-20; ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.27).** The served `GET /hsp/v1/openapi.json` document describes **PUBLIC endpoints only**; endpoints requiring authentication/capabilities (Doc 9 §22) are **EXCLUDED from the generated document**. The **exclusion is driven by the endpoint metadata auth field** (ADR-055 (c)), **not by route inspection** — registry-driven, consistent with ADR-055 (a). The **generator endpoint itself remains public and stateless** — **no capability check inside request-time generation** (consistent with ADR-055 (e)); it neither authenticates the caller nor branches output on caller identity. All six `hsp/v1` endpoints are public today, so the v1 MVP document describes all of them; the auth-field filter takes effect the moment any authenticated `hsp/v1` endpoint is registered, with no further ruling. **OAPI-S1 obligation:** the drift guard (ADR-055 (f)) must additionally assert **positively** that **non-public-metadata routes are ABSENT from the generated document** (exclusion test) — added to the OAPI-S1 DoD. Original text retained below for context. ADR-055 §(d) proposed the served OpenAPI 3.1 document be **public** (Doc 9 §22 — the described Pages/Posts/Categories endpoints are themselves public; Rule 6 treats the delivery API contract as consumer-facing); the open decision was **scoped to public endpoints only** vs **full contract behind a capability check**. All six `hsp/v1` endpoints are public today, so the MVP surface was unambiguous; the ruling above fixes the scoping for any future authenticated `hsp/v1` endpoint. The choice changes what an unauthenticated caller can discover about the API.

### FLAG-DOC8V2-2 — Doc 8 v2.0 introduces four `config/worker.php` processing-cycle keys not yet present in config

**Raised:** 2026-07-17 | **Session:** ARCH-DOC8-V2 | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-07-17 (ALIGN-S1).** The four keys ship in `config/worker.php` under `processing.*` — `relay_batch_size` (200), `dispatch_batch_size` (200), `projection_batch_size` (200), `cycle_time_budget_seconds` (20; well inside a typical `max_execution_time`), plus `schedule`/`interval_seconds` for the processing-cycle cron. The engine reads them (no hardcoded values): relay batch → `OutboxServiceProvider`, dispatch batch → `DispatcherServiceProvider`, projection batch + budget → the cycle-engine binding in `WorkerServiceProvider`; cron cadence → `ProcessingCronRegistrar` (DECISION R config-driven-cadence precedent). No schema change. Original text retained below for context. ADR-054 / Doc 8 v2.0 specify four config-driven, no-hardcoded-value knobs in `config/worker.php` — `processing.relay_batch_size`, `processing.dispatch_batch_size`, `processing.projection_batch_size`, `processing.cycle_time_budget_seconds` (sensible defaults; **no schema change**).

### FLAG-ALIGN-1 — Per-cycle processing-component identity (UUIDv7) + heartbeat status value set under the ADR-054 cycle model

**Raised:** 2026-07-17 | **Session:** ALIGN-S0 | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-07-17 (DECISION X, ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.24; architect ruling 2026-07-17; implemented ALIGN-S1).** (a) worker identity = **Option A** — each cycle mints a **fresh UUIDv7** at bootstrap; `system.worker_heartbeats` rows are processing-cycle executions (one row per recent cycle/stage), stale rows pruned under existing retention; this cardinality makes DECISION Q on-demand cycle metrics (cycles_completed/avg_cycle_duration — ALIGN-S2 T4) feasible. (b) status set = **exactly `'running'`/`'idle'`** (`'processing'`→`'running'`; `'shutdown'` removed). (c) `WorkerInterface` = **Option A architectural correction** — internal core contract; `run()`/`shutdown()` **removed**; the contract expresses exactly one bounded processing cycle returning a processing result. Built in ALIGN-S1 (cycle engine `ProcessingCycleResult`/`WorkerInterface::runCycle()`, fresh-UUID heartbeats, `running`/`idle` set, per-strategy daemon bindings retired). Original text retained below for context. **The shipped code assigned `worker_id` once at construction; DECISION X supersedes that.** Doc 8 v2.0 §8/§15/§24 + ADR-054 §8 define `worker_id` as a **per-cycle** UUIDv7 self-assigned at cycle bootstrap, and §16 defines the status set as `'running'`/`'idle'` (removing `starting/recycling/stopping/failed`; ADR-054 removes `'shutdown'`). The shipped code assigns `worker_id` **once at construction** and "never changes during the worker lifetime" ([WorkerEngine.php:159](headless-sync/core/Workers/WorkerEngine.php#L159); [WorkerInterface.php:11](headless-sync/core/Contracts/WorkerInterface.php#L11); [RelayWorkerStrategy.php:55](headless-sync/core/Workers/Strategies/RelayWorkerStrategy.php#L55)) and emits `'processing'`/`'idle'`/`'shutdown'` ([HeartbeatRecord.php:24](headless-sync/core/Workers/HeartbeatRecord.php#L24), [WorkerEngine.php:83](headless-sync/core/Workers/WorkerEngine.php#L83)). **Ruling needed:** (a) does each cron cycle mint a FRESH UUIDv7 (so `system.worker_heartbeats` holds one row per recent cycle — which is what makes `worker_count` = "cycles/stages that ran recently", ADR-054 §6), or a STABLE per-stage-per-schedule id (keeps the current-state table small)? The two give different `worker_count`/health semantics. (b) Confirm the status set is exactly `'running'`/`'idle'`. Not improvised — changes heartbeat row cardinality and every derived worker/health/metric signal. See `docs/ADR054-IMPLEMENTATION-AUDIT.md` §6.

### FLAG-ALIGN-2 — DECISION W (c) backfill "live worker heartbeat" prerequisite under cycle-freshness heartbeats

**Raised:** 2026-07-17 | **Session:** ALIGN-S0 | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-07-17 (DECISION X, ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.24, ruling (4) = Option C; architect ruling 2026-07-17).** The DECISION W (c) prerequisite is satisfied by **BOTH** (i) the processing-cycle WP-Cron event being **scheduled** (`wp_next_scheduled` truthy) **AND** (ii) a **recent processing heartbeat** (a cycle ran within the offline threshold — the existing DECISION P age read). Remediation references **only WP-Cron** ("ensure WP-Cron is firing / run `wp cron event run --due-now` / check the processing cron is scheduled"), never supervisor/systemd/daemon/"restart the worker". `BackfillService` no-in-request-drain + re-emission repair unchanged. **The `BackfillGate` realignment (remediation rewrite + Option-C gate) is implemented in ALIGN-S2**, not ALIGN-S1 (BackfillGate untouched here). Original text retained below for context. DECISION W (c) makes "a live worker heartbeat a HARD PREREQUISITE" for backfill; `BackfillGate::workerGate()` remediation currently says "Start (or restart) the HSP worker via your process supervisor (systemd / Supervisor / container) … the supervisor owns worker lifecycle" ([BackfillGate.php:101-105](headless-sync/core/Onboarding/Backfill/BackfillGate.php#L101-L105)). Under ADR-054 §5 there is **no supervised worker** — a fresh `system.worker_heartbeats` row means "a processing CYCLE ran recently," and a stale/missing one means "**cron may not be firing**," not "a daemon is down." **Ruling needed:** what satisfies the prerequisite under the cycle model — (a) a fresh cycle heartbeat within the offline threshold (the existing age read), (b) proof the processing-cycle cron event is scheduled + WP-Cron enabled, or (c) both — and what the remediation should say ("ensure WP-Cron is firing / run `wp cron event run`", not "restart the daemon"). The `BackfillService` no-in-request-drain design + re-emission repair path are preserved either way; only the gate's MEANING + remediation need the ruling. Not improvised. See `docs/ADR054-IMPLEMENTATION-AUDIT.md` §6.

### FLAG-ALIGNS1-1 — `Application::activate()` → `ProcessingCronRegistrar::ensureScheduled()` wiring verified by inspection only

**Raised:** 2026-07-18 | **Session:** ALIGN-S1 | **Status:** **RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (ALIGN-S2) — verified against live MySQL (127.0.0.1:10053) + live PG (127.0.0.1:5432), both tests EXECUTED (not skipped) and PASSED, deterministic on two runs.** Added `tests/Integration/Bootstrap/ApplicationActivationSchedulingTest` — a live-MySQL+PG smoke test that drives the **real** `Application::activate()` (boots the full container, resolves the runtime PG handles, then schedules the events) and asserts `wp_next_scheduled('hsp_processing_cycle')` becomes truthy (plus the three reconciliation events), with an idempotency case proving re-activation does not double-schedule. It self-skips when the DBs are absent (integration tier) and resets the `Application` singleton via reflection so it does not leak a booted instance. **Finding (first real `activate()` drive):** booting the genuine container resolves the `relay.worker` binding, which reads **`$wpdb->prefix`** ([OutboxServiceProvider.php:97](headless-sync/core/Container/Definitions/OutboxServiceProvider.php#L97)) — a headless PHPUnit process never loads WordPress, so `global $wpdb` is null there and the first run ERRORED with "Attempt to read property `prefix` on null". This is not a DB-availability problem (the DBs were up); the container graph genuinely requires a WordPress `$wpdb` global. **Resolution (architect ruling 2026-07-18, Option 1 — test-only):** the test provides a minimal **test-scoped `$GLOBALS['wpdb']` stub exposing only `->prefix`** (set in setUp, restored in tearDown; NOT added to `tests/bootstrap.php`) — the single WP substitution, analogous to how `ProcessingCycleIntegrationTest` substitutes the WP state-reload boundary. No production code changed. With the stub, both tests pass and NO further WP-absent surface appeared on the `activate()` path (the only `$wpdb` deref is `relay.worker`'s `$wpdb->prefix`). The `activate() → boot() → ProcessingCronRegistrar → ensureScheduled → wp_next_scheduled` wiring under test stays GENUINE — registrar-level coverage was NOT substituted. **Principle 8 (Zero-Configuration Operation — "activate and synchronization begins") is now proven end-to-end**, not by inspection. *Original analysis retained below for context.* The `Application::activate()` → `ProcessingCronRegistrar::ensureScheduled()` wiring is verified by **code inspection only** ([Application.php:71-72](headless-sync/bootstrap/Application.php#L71)). The scheduling *behaviour* is registrar-tested — `ProcessingCronRegistrarTest::test_register_schedules_the_processing_event_when_not_scheduled` + `::test_register_is_idempotent_does_not_double_schedule` prove `ensureScheduled()` schedules `hsp_processing_cycle` and honours the `wp_next_scheduled`-truthy guard — and `deactivate()` is tested against the **real** `Application` (`ApplicationCronSchedulingTest::test_deactivate_clears_all_scheduled_hsp_cron_events`). But **no test exercises the `activate()` entry point itself** (it boots the full container/DBs, so it was not unit-tested).

### FLAG-GATES3-1 — GATE-S3 criterion 3 (Reconciliation) is unsatisfiable: reconciliation is a stub, never built

**Raised:** 2026-07-13 | **Session:** GATE-S3 (pre-implementation) | **Status:** **RESOLVED — DECISION U (ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.18, 2026-07-13); Option A ratified.** OPS-S3 "Reconciliation MVP" is inserted in the Session Map immediately before GATE-S3; GATE-S3 Depends-on changes to OPS-S3; GATE-S3 DoD is UNCHANGED. Reconciliation un-stubs `ReconciliationWorkerStrategy` and repairs delivery drift ONLY via the DECISION T re-emission primitive (`ReplayService`/`ReplayEmitterInterface`) — direct PG projection writes prohibited; WordPress-wins holds by construction (ADR-026/027/045). Scope: drift detection + incremental validation + full reconciliation, detecting (a) missed captures (WP newer/absent vs delivery — DECISION 1 backstop) and (b) orphans (present in delivery, deleted in WP → re-emit `.deleted` → DECISION I tombstone). Scheduling: WP-Cron authorized (recovery-jobs carve-out) to *trigger* only; workers remain the execution path. Design ratified in-chat (DECISION U v1.19 — three modes, D2 taxonomy existence-only, D4 suppression rule, D5 executor B1, D6 core contract, D7 unbounded paged full sweep) and **BUILT + tested in OPS-S3 (2026-07-13):** un-stubbed strategy façade over `ReconciliationService`; 25 unit + 6 live-DB integration tests green; full suite 923 green on two cold runs. GATE-S3 criterion 3 is now satisfiable. The original flag analysis is retained below for context.

**STOP condition hit before writing any gate test. No production code was changed and no gate test was written.** GATE-S3 (Operability Validation) has three §4 criteria. Criteria 1 (worker health / failure detection within one heartbeat cycle — DECISION P) and 2 (DLQ payload snapshot + stack trace — OPEN-3) are backed by real production code and are validatable. **Criterion 3 (Reconciliation) cannot be validated because the production behavior it must exercise does not exist.**

**The gap:** GATE-S3 criterion 3 requires: *"Reconciliation executes: hourly drift detection, incremental validation, and full reconciliation (ADR-026); on divergence, WordPress state always wins and PG is repaired — never the reverse (ADR-027, ADR-045)."* Against the current tree:

1. `ReconciliationWorkerStrategy::execute()` ([ReconciliationWorkerStrategy.php:23-27](headless-sync/core/Workers/Strategies/ReconciliationWorkerStrategy.php#L23-L27)) is a **STUB** — it returns `false` and performs no drift detection and no repair. Its own comment states domain logic is "implemented in OPS-S1 / Phase 3 Operational Hardening."
2. It is registered as a stub in the DI container ([WorkerServiceProvider.php:113](headless-sync/core/Container/Definitions/WorkerServiceProvider.php#L113), doc-comment line 36 labels it "stub").
3. A full-codebase search (`core/`, `modules/`) finds **no** drift-detection routine, **no** incremental validation, **no** full reconciliation, and **no** WordPress-wins repair path. There is **no** `hsp reconcile` CLI command (the `hsp` surface has DLQ + replay only), **no** scheduled/cron wiring, and **no** reconciliation service. ADR-026 (Doc 4 §886) requires a schedule — Hourly Drift Detection / Nightly Incremental Validation / Weekly Full Reconciliation — none of which is wired.

**Why this is a STOP, not a fix (per the GATE-S3 brief):** The brief states *"NO production code changes"* and *"If a validation check fails, that is a STOP-and-flag, not a fix."* Building reconciliation is production code and is out of GATE-S3 scope. The DoD also forbids `markTestIncomplete`. Writing gate evidence against absent behavior would require either improvising the reconciliation mechanism (prohibited — no DECISION specifies drift-detection scope, cadence driver, or repair granularity beyond the DECISION T primitive) or a placeholder test (disallowed). Neither is permitted.

**Sequencing conflict (root cause):** The IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §5b Session Map places **GATE-S3** (which requires reconciliation to *execute*) before any session that *builds* reconciliation. OPS-S1's scope (Session Map line 380) shipped heartbeat + DLQ + replay + metrics — **not** reconciliation. Phase 3 "Operational Hardening" (IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §5), where reconciliation is deferred, is post-MVP and unscheduled. The frozen docs corroborate reconciliation as future work: DECISION T ([ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md:792](docs/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md#L792)) — *"...future reconciliation (ADR-026/ADR-027/ADR-045) will use... Reconciliation sessions build on DECISION T rather than inventing a second repair path."* No reconciliation session has been run. GATE-S3 criterion 3 is therefore unsatisfiable as sequenced.

**What is NOT in dispute:** Criteria 1 and 2 are ready to validate. This flag blocks only the reconciliation criterion — and, because the gate is all-or-nothing, GATE-S3 completion overall.

**Options (architect must choose ONE — none may be implemented without a DECISION):**

- **Option A — Insert a reconciliation build session before GATE-S3.** Add an `OPS-S3` (or `RECON-S1`) session that un-stubs `ReconciliationWorkerStrategy`: drift detection (compare WordPress source-of-truth against `content.*` projections), incremental validation, full reconciliation, and WordPress-wins repair — built on the DECISION T emit-through-outbox primitive (per ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md line 792). Requires the architect to specify: drift-detection query/scope, the three cadences' scheduling driver (WP-Cron fallback vs. worker vs. external), and repair granularity. GATE-S3 then validates real behavior afterward. This is the path most consistent with the frozen "reconciliation sessions build on DECISION T" ruling.
- **Option B — Descope GATE-S3 criterion 3 to Phase 3.** Rule that Operability Validation for the Blog MVP gate covers criteria 1 + 2 only (heartbeat/failure detection + DLQ diagnostics), and that reconciliation validation moves to Phase 3 Operational Hardening where reconciliation is actually built. Amend IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §4 Operability Validation and the GATE-S3 DoD accordingly. GATE-S3 then passes on the two implemented criteria.
- **Option C — Reduced-scope reconciliation for the gate.** Ratify a minimal reconciliation implementation sufficient for the gate (e.g. on-demand full reconciliation via a `hsp reconcile` command using DECISION T re-emission, deferring the hourly/nightly/weekly *scheduling* to Phase 3), and authorize it as an in-scope build. *Tension:* still requires a production build (currently out of GATE-S3 scope) and a DECISION defining the reduced surface.

**Resolution trigger:** Architect selects one option, records it as a DECISION in `docs/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md`, and authorizes any session/scope/schema change it entails. Only then can GATE-S3 proceed. Until then, GATE-S3 criteria 1 and 2 evidence may be written, but GATE-S3 as a whole remains BLOCKED and the Operability item stays unchecked.

### FLAG-OPSS2-1 — Entity + date-range replay vs. DECISION J stale guard: reprojection mechanism unspecified

**Raised:** 2026-07-12 | **Session:** OPS-S2 (pre-implementation) | **Status:** **RESOLVED — DECISION T (ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md v1.17, 2026-07-12); Option A ratified and built in OPS-S2.**

**Resolution (architect ratified Option A → DECISION T):** Replay is **projection repair via synthetic re-emission**. Entity and date-range replay emit a NEW event through `wp_hsp_outbox` taking a NEW `aggregate_version` from `wp_hsp_aggregate_counters` (DECISION 2), flowing relay → dispatch → worker and passing the DECISION J stale guard **naturally** (new version > stored) — the guard is not weakened, bypassed, or made conditional. Historical `system.events` rows are never mutated or re-enqueued (Doc 5 §26 holds; replay appends). The emitter reads CURRENT WordPress state (ADR-044/ADR-045): exists+public → `.updated`; missing/non-public → `.deleted` (tombstones outage-window deletions). Date-range mode: `SELECT DISTINCT (aggregate_type, aggregate_id)` from `system.events` in `[from, to)` via the existing delivery handle (no fifth handle). Traceability: one `correlation_id` per run, `causation_id` per operation. No schema change; no new pg_* wrapper; no new PG handle. The IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §5b "re-enqueue original event" wording is superseded for entity/date-range modes; single-event DLQ replay (DECISION S) is unchanged. Built in OPS-S2 (see Session Log).

**STOP condition hit at pre-implementation (retained for context). No replay code was written before the ruling.** OPS-S2 was scoped to un-stub `ReplayWorkerStrategy` and build entity + date-range replay (Doc 4 §24). Before writing code, the brief required determining how Doc 4 §24 specifies replay to interact with the DECISION J stale guard. It does not — the mechanism is unspecified across all frozen docs, and the three governing constraints are mutually incompatible for the already-processed case.

**The conflict (three frozen constraints that cannot all hold):**

1. **Replay re-enqueues the original event version** — IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §5b line 284: *"Replay re-enqueues the original event version; does not mutate historical contracts (Doc 5 §26)."* Doc 5 §26: *"Replay must use original event versions… Replay must never mutate historical contracts."*
2. **DECISION J stale guard is binding and may not be weakened/bypassed** — `EventWorkerStrategy::isStale()` ([EventWorkerStrategy.php:205](headless-sync/core/Workers/Strategies/EventWorkerStrategy.php#L205)): `event.aggregate_version <= stored latest_processed_version → stale → ack, ZERO writes`. Per the brief, this guard *"may not be weakened, bypassed, or made conditional without a DECISION."*
3. **DoD criterion 1/2 requires the entity to reproject to correct final state.**

For an aggregate that was **already successfully processed**, `system.aggregate_versions.latest_processed_version` already equals the newest event's `aggregate_version`. Re-enqueuing any existing `event_id` for that aggregate → the DECISION J guard sees `version <= stored` → **acks with zero projection writes**. The entity does **not** reproject. Constraints 1 + 2 + 3 cannot simultaneously hold.

**Why DLQ replay (DECISION S / OPS-S1) does NOT resolve this:** DLQ replay works precisely because a dead-lettered event's aggregate was *never successfully processed* — its version is still **ahead** of `latest_processed_version`, so the guard lets it through (DECISION S clause (c) even documents the "already-current → zero writes" case as acceptable *for that path*). Entity/date-range replay targets **already-current** aggregates, which is the opposite situation: the guard suppresses them.

**Why Doc 5 §26 does not disambiguate:** §26 governs `event_version` (the contract/schema version — "v1 stays v1"), a field distinct from the per-aggregate `aggregate_version` monotonic counter (Doc 5 lines 262 vs 268). §26 forbids rewriting event schema versions and mutating historical event contracts; it says nothing about the `aggregate_version`↔stale-guard interaction. IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md line 284's "original event version" is itself ambiguous between the two readings, and neither reading specifies the reprojection mechanism.

**Options (architect must choose ONE — I have NOT picked one, and none may be implemented without a DECISION):**

- **Option A — Fresh synthetic event via the outbox with a new counter version.** Replay re-captures current WP state (ADR-044/DECISION H) into `wp_hsp_outbox`, allocating a new `aggregate_version` via `wp_hsp_aggregate_counters` (DECISION 2). The new version is `> latest_processed_version`, so it passes the guard naturally and reprojects. *Tension:* appears to violate constraint 1 ("re-enqueues the **original** event version") if that phrase binds `aggregate_version`; must confirm §26 only binds `event_version`. Honors Rule 3 (goes through the outbox) and ADR-044 (reload current state). Requires no schema change.
- **Option B — Guard bypass for replay.** A replay-originated job carries a flag that instructs `EventWorkerStrategy` to skip the Resolve-stage guard (Layer 2 GREATEST guard still holds). *Tension:* directly weakens/makes-conditional the DECISION J guard — explicitly prohibited by the brief without a DECISION; also risks a replay racing a live higher-version event.
- **Option C — Version-state reset.** Before re-enqueue, delete/lower the `system.aggregate_versions` row for the target aggregate(s) so the original event is no longer `<=` stored. *Tension:* mutates version state (arguably "mutates historical" state), interacts with the GREATEST guard and `system.processed_events`, and risks regression flagged by DoD criterion 4.

**Also unspecified (dependent on the above):** whether date-range replay enumerates affected aggregates from `system.events` by `created_at`/`source_updated_at` window, or from another source; and the WP-CLI surface shape (`hsp replay entity <type> <id>` / `hsp replay range <from> <to>` — extending the OPS-S1 `hsp` command surface per the brief).

**Schema note:** If the ratified mechanism requires any new table or column (e.g. a replay-marker column, a replay-batch table), that is a **separate frozen-schema change requiring its own DECISION** before implementation — per the brief's out-of-scope clause. Option A requires none; Options B/C may.

**Resolution trigger:** Architect ratifies the replay↔stale-guard mechanism (one of the options above or another), records it as a DECISION in `docs/ARCHITECTURE_DECISIONS.md`, and authorizes any schema change it entails. Only then may OPS-S2 build entity + date-range replay. GATE-S1 criterion 2 remains BLOCKED (FLAG-GATES1-1) until OPS-S2 ships under that ruling.

### FLAG-P0S1-1 — PSR-11 stubs bundled as local source files

**Raised:** 2026-06-22 | **Session:** P0-S1

P0-S1 scope forbids `require`/`require-dev` entries in `composer.json`. The Container
implements `Psr\Container\ContainerInterface` (PSR-11). To satisfy both constraints,
`Psr\Container` interfaces are bundled as three stub files under `core/Psr/Container/`,
mapped via an additional `psr-4` entry in `composer.json`.

**Impact:** When `psr/container` is added as a proper composer dependency in a later session,
the stub files and the `"Psr\\Container\\"` `psr-4` entry in `composer.json` must be removed
to avoid class redeclaration errors. This is a one-line composer require + delete of three
files + remove the composer.json psr-4 line.

**Resolution trigger:** This flag is resolved when the project introduces the official runtime
`psr/container` package via Composer. At that point: remove the temporary `core/Psr/Container/`
interfaces; remove the associated temporary PSR-4 mapping; refactor the Container to depend on
the official package; verify the platform boots and all tests pass. `require-dev` tooling
(e.g. phpunit) does NOT trip this trigger.

**Raised:** 2026-06-22 | **Session:** P0-S4 | **Status:** Resolved — P0-S4 follow-up (2026-06-22)

**Resolution:** Redesigned by removing the intermediate status entirely. `RelayWorkerStrategy` now holds the MySQL `FOR UPDATE` row lock for the entire batch duration: `BEGIN` → SELECT FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED → (PG insert + MySQL mark-`'relayed'` per row) → `COMMIT`. The row lock is the claim guard; no status transition to `'relaying'` is needed. OPEN-6 v1.3 frozen `ENUM('pending','relayed')` DDL is correct as-is — no migration change. 91/91 unit tests pass; a negative-assertion test (`test_tick_does_not_use_relaying_intermediate_status`) confirms `'relaying'` never appears in emitted SQL.

**Raised:** 2026-06-22 | **Session:** P0-S4 close | **Status:** Resolved — P0-S7 (2026-06-22)

**Resolution:** (1) `RelayWorkerStrategy::insertIntoSystemEvents()` already appended `'+00:00'` to both `source_updated_at` and `created_at` bindings (`$row['created_at'] . '+00:00'`), cast via `$12::timestamptz`. PostgreSQL interprets the `+00:00` suffix as UTC, guaranteeing the stored TIMESTAMPTZ reflects capture time in UTC regardless of session timezone. (2) `RelayEndToEndTest::test_pending_row_is_relayed_and_marked_relayed` strengthened: now asserts the full captured UTC datetime string (`assertStringContainsString($captureUtc, ...)`) AND that the PG value ends with an explicit UTC offset (`assertMatchesRegularExpression('/\+00(:00)?$/', ...)`). The `insertOutboxRow()` helper gains an optional `captureAt` parameter so the test can pin the timestamp before insertion.
